Be thorough. For EVERY item, check:
A. Logic: is the keyed answer correct (indicator, its group, the complete sufficient and necessary conditions with every qualification, the diagram direction, the if/then rewrite, the contrapositive with correct negations and double-negative handling)? Is any accepted answer wrong, or any correct answer rejected by the grader? If grading is text matching, find realistic correct student answers that would be marked wrong and wrong ones marked right.
B. Feedback/explanations: does each explanation match the item and the key? Flag generic, wrong, or mismatched explanations.
C. Sequencing: does a drill require something not yet taught at its position (e.g. contrapositive before the Contrapositive lesson at step 15; "only after", "unless", "without" if never taught; Group 2 words before the necessary-words lesson)?
D. Walkthrough demos (drillDemos.ts): Joshua's rule is every drill gets its own animated walkthrough of that drill's real steps with a fresh example, never a shared demo. E. Copy rules (Joshua's standing rules): no em dashes or en dashes in student copy; no "~" squiggle for negation (a diagonal strike or the word "not"); banned examples: raffle/bicycle, coat, coffee, breathing, elevator; "door"/"doors" never as a unit of content; no "patterns"/"move" as student words; no "promise", "depends on", "nothing to stand on"; no stock encouragement; no Logic Games mentions; no internal production labels. F. Behaviour/state bugs: progress/resume keyed by position vs id; completion that fires before the student finishes; items that can't be completed; stale state on reset or when switching drills (e.g. revisit drills sharing state with the original drills: can a revisit overwrite or corrupt the original drill's saved answers?); duplicate React keys; inputs without labels; focus handling; very long drills with no save point (Drill 7 claims "work in short sessions and return where you left off": verify that is actually true).
G. Duplication across drills and with lesson examples.

Do not report style nits that don't affect students. 140	   140	  const stageIndex = Math.max(0, Math.min(answer.stage, stages.length - 1));

142	   142	  const stage = stages[stageIndex];

144	   144	  const revisit = set.items.filter(i => saved.answers[i.id]?.review === 'revisit').length;
145	   145	  const percent = Math.round(reviewed / set.items.length * 100);

157	   157	  const contraText = production && answer.contraLeft && answer.contraRight ? `${answer.negateLeft ? 'NOT ' : ''}${symbolFor(answer.contraLeft)} → ${answer.negateRight ? 'NOT ' : ''}${symbolFor(answer.contraRight)}` : '';

159	   159	  const contraOrderCorrect = production ? answer.contraLeft === necessaryKey && answer.contraRight === sufficientKey : answer.contraLeft === 'necessary' && answer.contraRight === 'sufficient';
160	   160	  const contraCorrect = contraOrderCorrect && answer.negateLeft && answer.negateRight;
161	   161	  const contraHint = !contraOrderCorrect ? 'Start with the right side of your original diagram. It becomes the new left side.' : 'The order is right. Now negate each whole side once.';

166	   166	  const correct = stage === 'indicators' ? indicatorsCorrect : stage === 'conditions' ? spanCheck.correct : stage === 'diagram' ? placementCorrect : stage === 'contra' ? contraCorrect : true;

168	   168	  const deferredNote = stage === 'symbols' ? '' : set.taskKind === 'contrapositive' ? 'You compare your wording with ours at the end.' : 'Our version stays hidden until you have completed the whole translation.';

176	   176	    if (started) record?.({ state: saved, done: finishedItems, total: set.items.length, ...(finishedItems === set.items.length ? { status: 'done' as const } : {}) });

247	   247	function Frame({ frame }: { frame: DemoFrame }) {
248	   248	  if (frame.kind === 'pick') {
249	   249	    let order = 0;

251	   251	      <p className="dd-sentence">{frame.words.map((word, index) => {
252	   252	        const picked = frame.picks.includes(index);
253	   253	        const delay = picked ? 0.7 + 0.7 * order++ : 0;
254	   254	        return <span key={index} className={picked ? 'dd-word dd-picked' : 'dd-word'} style={picked ? { animationDelay: `${delay}s` } : undefined}>{word}</span>;
255	   255	      })}</p>
256	   256	      <span className="dd-chip" style={{ animationDelay: `${0.9 + 0.7 * frame.picks.length}s` }}>{frame.chip}</span>
257	   257	    </div>;
258	   258	  }
259	   259	  if (frame.kind === 'type') return <div className="dd-type">{frame.lines.map((line, index) => <div key={index} className="dd-line">
260	   260	    {line.label && <span className="dd-label">{line.label}</span>}
261	   261	    <span className="dd-field"><span className="dd-typed" style={{ animationDelay: `${0.4 + index * 1.4}s` }}>{line.text}</span></span>
262	   262	  </div>)}</div>;
263	   263	  if (frame.kind === 'place') return <div className="dd-place">
264	   264	    <div className="dd-slot"><span className="dd-label">Enough (left)</span><span className="dd-card dd-card-left">{frame.left}</span></div>
265	   265	    <span className="dd-arrow" aria-hidden="true">→</span>
266	   266	    <div className="dd-slot"><span className="dd-label">Required (right)</span><span className="dd-card dd-card-right">{frame.right}</span></div>
267	   267	  </div>;
268	   268	  return <div className="dd-choose">
269	   269	    <p className="dd-question">{frame.question}</p>
270	   270	    <div className="dd-options">{frame.options.map((option, index) => <span key={index} className={index === frame.correct ? 'dd-option dd-correct' : 'dd-option'}>
271	   271	      <i>{index === frame.correct ? <Check size={13}/> : String.fromCharCode(65 + index)}</i>{option}
272	   272	    </span>)}</div>
273	   273	  </div>;
274	   274	}

276	   276	/** Instruction-page walkthrough: one animated frame per step of this drill, in drill order. */
277	   277	export function DrillDemo({ drillId }: { drillId: string }) {
278	   278	  const demo = drillDemos[drillId];
279	   279	  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);
280	   280	  const [paused, setPaused] = useState(reducedMotion);
281	   281	  useEffect(() => {
282	   282	    if (!demo || paused) return;
283	   283	    const timer = setTimeout(() => setIndex(current => (current + 1) % demo.steps.length), STEP_MS);
284	   284	    return () => clearTimeout(timer);
285	   285	  }, [demo, index, paused]);
286	   286	  if (!demo) return null;
287	   287	  const step = demo.steps[index];
288	   288	  return <section className="dd-demo" aria-label="How this drill works">
289	   289	    <p className="dd-eyebrow">How this drill works</p>
290	   290	    {demo.sentence && <blockquote className="dd-example">Example: {demo.sentence}</blockquote>}
291	   291	    <ol className="dd-steps">{demo.steps.map((item, i) => <li key={item.title}>
292	   292	      <button type="button" aria-current={i === index ? 'step' : undefined} onClick={() => { setIndex(i); setPaused(true); }}><span>{i + 1}</span>{item.title}</button>
293	   293	    </li>)}</ol>
294	   294	    <div className="dd-stage" key={index} aria-live="polite">
295	   295	      <p className="dd-caption"><strong>Step {index + 1}.</strong> {step.caption}</p>
296	   296	      <Frame frame={step.frame}/>
297	   297	    </div>
298	   298	    {demo.steps.length > 1 && <div className="dd-progress" aria-hidden="true"><i key={`${index}-${paused}`} className={paused ? 'dd-bar-paused' : ''} style={{ animationDuration: `${STEP_MS}ms` }}/></div>}
299	   299	  </section>;
300	   300	}

304	   304	 * is not one of the drill's own questions.

306	   306	export type DemoFrame =
307	   307	  | { kind: 'pick'; words: string[]; picks: number[]; chip: string }
308	   308	  | { kind: 'type'; lines: { label?: string; text: string }[] }
309	   309	  | { kind: 'place'; left: string; right: string }
310	   310	  | { kind: 'choose'; question: string; options: string[]; correct: number };

315	   315	const words = (sentence: string) => sentence.split(' ');
316	   316	
317	   317	function contrapositiveDemo(sentence: string, picks: number[], chip: string, one: string, two: string, s1: string, s2: string, ifThen: string, contraWords: string): DrillDemo {
318	   318	  return { sentence, steps: [
319	   319	    { title: 'Find the indicators', caption: 'Click each indicator word, then choose its group.', frame: { kind: 'pick', words: words(sentence), picks, chip } },
320	   320	    { title: 'Keep the complete ideas', caption: 'Click the first and last word of each condition. Leave the indicator out.', frame: { kind: 'pick', words: words(sentence), picks: words(sentence).map((_, i) => i).filter(i => !picks.includes(i)), chip: 'Both conditions marked' } },
321	   321	    { title: 'Define your symbols', caption: 'Give each condition its own letter, and reuse it.', frame: { kind: 'type', lines: [{ text: `${s1} = ${one.toLowerCase()}` }, { text: `${s2} = ${two.toLowerCase()}` }] } },
322	   322	    { title: 'Build the diagram', caption: 'Put your letters in the slots: what is enough on the left, what it requires on the right.', frame: { kind: 'type', lines: [{ label: 'Your diagram', text: `${s1} → ${s2}` }] } },
323	   323	    { title: 'Reverse and negate', caption: 'Switch the sides, then turn NOT on for both.', frame: { kind: 'type', lines: [{ label: 'Original', text: `${s1} → ${s2}` }, { label: 'Contrapositive', text: `NOT ${s2} → NOT ${s1}` }] } },
324	   324	    { title: 'Read the original', caption: 'Write the original rule as an if/then sentence.', frame: { kind: 'type', lines: [{ text: ifThen }] } },
325	   325	    { title: 'Read the contrapositive', caption: 'Write the contrapositive in plain words.', frame: { kind: 'type', lines: [{ text: contraWords }] } },
326	   326	  ] };
327	   327	}

380	   380	export function OpeningActivityPractice() {
381	   381	  return (
382	   382	    <section className="cir-root opening-activity-practice" aria-label="Build diagrams from fresh sentences">
383	   383	      <p className="opening-activity-intro">Read the rule and put each complete idea on the appropriate side. Then read your diagram as an if/then statement. Does it keep the original meaning?</p>
384	   384	      <DiagramDrills
385	   385	        items={openingActivityItems}
386	   386	        explanations={openingActivityExplanations}
387	   387	        persistProgress={false}
388	   388	        allowContrapositive={false}
389	   389	        allowNegation={false}
390	   390	        shuffleItems={false}
391	   391	      />
392	   392	    </section>
393	   393	  );
394	   394	}
395	   395	import { useEffect, useId, useRef, useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';
396	   396	
397	   397	export type PracticeRound = {
398	   398	  title: string;
399	   399	  tasks: Array<{ title: string; content: ReactNode }>;
400	   400	};
401	   401	
402	   402	/** Navigation is formative. Moving between tasks never records completion. */
403	   403	export function PracticeTaskRunner({ rounds }: { rounds: PracticeRound[] }) {
404	   404	  const [current, setCurrent] = useState(0);
405	   405	  const [moveFocus, setMoveFocus] = useState(false);
406	   406	  const heading = useRef<HTMLHeadingElement>(null);
407	   407	  const id = useId();
408	   408	  const tasks = rounds.flatMap((round, roundIndex) => round.tasks.map((task, taskIndex) => ({
409	   409	    ...task, roundIndex, taskIndex,
410	   410	  })));
411	   411	  const task = tasks[current];
412	   412	  useEffect(() => {
413	   413	    if (!moveFocus) return;
414	   414	    heading.current?.focus();
415	   415	    setMoveFocus(false);
416	   416	  }, [current, moveFocus]);
417	   417	  function go(index: number) {
418	   418	    setCurrent(index);
419	   419	    setMoveFocus(true);
420	   420	  }
421	   421	  return <div className="co-task-runner">
422	   422	    {rounds.length > 1 && <nav className="co-round-links" aria-label="Practice rounds">
423	   423	      {rounds.map((round, index) => <button type="button" key={round.title}
424	   424	        aria-pressed={task.roundIndex === index}
425	   425	        onClick={() => go(tasks.findIndex(item => item.roundIndex === index))}>
426	   426	        {index + 1}. {round.title}
427	   427	      </button>)}
428	   428	    </nav>}
429	   429	    <div className="co-task-header">
430	   430	      <p className="co-kicker" role="status">{rounds.length > 1 && <>Round {task.roundIndex + 1} of {rounds.length} · </>}Task {task.taskIndex + 1} of {rounds[task.roundIndex].tasks.length}</p>
431	   431	      <h2 id={`${id}-heading`} ref={heading} tabIndex={-1}>{task.title}</h2>
432	   432	      <progress value={current + 1} max={tasks.length} aria-label="Current practice task"/>
433	   433	    </div>
434	   434	    {tasks.map((item, index) => <div key={`${item.roundIndex}-${item.taskIndex}`} hidden={current !== index}
435	   435	      role="region" aria-label={item.title} data-practice-task={index + 1}>
436	   436	      {item.content}
437	   437	    </div>)}
438	   438	    <nav className="co-task-turn" aria-label="Task navigation">
439	   439	      <button type="button" disabled={current === 0} onClick={() => go(current - 1)}>Previous task</button>
440	   440	      <button type="button" disabled={current === tasks.length - 1} onClick={() => go(current + 1)}>Next task</button>
441	   441	    </nav>
442	   442	  </div>;
443	   443	}

95	    95	function CardStatus({ saved, drill }: { saved?: CourseProgressStore['steps'][string]; drill: boolean }) {
96	    96	  if (!saved) return <span className="oc-status oc-status-new">Not started</span>;
97	    97	  if (saved.status === 'done') return <span className="oc-status oc-status-done"><Check size={13} aria-hidden/>Done</span>;
98	    98	  const partial = drill && saved.total ? <>{saved.done ?? 0} / {saved.total}<span className="oc-status-bar" aria-hidden><i style={{ width: `${(saved.done ?? 0) / saved.total * 100}%` }}/></span></> : 'In progress';
99	    99	  return <span className="oc-status oc-status-started">{partial}</span>;
100	   100	}

102	   102	function CourseTracker({ steps }: { steps: OpeningCourseStep[] }) {
103	   103	  const saved = useCourseProgress()?.steps ?? {};
104	   104	  const done = steps.filter(step => saved[step.id]?.status === 'done').length;
105	   105	  return <div className="oc-tracker" role="group" aria-label="Your progress">
106	   106	    <div className="oc-tracker-top"><span>Your progress</span><strong>{done} <small>of {steps.length} done</small></strong></div>
107	   107	    <div className="oc-tracker-steps">{steps.map(step => <i key={step.id} data-progress={saved[step.id]?.status ?? 'new'} data-type={step.type} title={step.title}/>)}</div>
108	   108	  </div>;
109	   109	}

111	   111	/** Marks a lesson started on arrival and done once its closing navigation is reached. */
112	   112	function LessonCompletion({ stepId }: { stepId: string }) {
113	   113	  const store = useCourseProgress();
114	   114	  const save = store?.save;
115	   115	  useEffect(() => {
116	   116	    if (!save) return;
117	   117	    save(stepId, {});
118	   118	    const end = document.querySelector('.oc-course-turn');
119	   119	    if (!end || typeof IntersectionObserver === 'undefined') return;
120	   120	    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {
121	   121	      if (entries.some(entry => entry.isIntersecting)) { save(stepId, { status: 'done' }); observer.disconnect(); }
122	   122	    });
123	   123	    observer.observe(end);
124	   124	    return () => observer.disconnect();
125	   125	  }, [save, stepId]);
126	   126	  return null;
127	   127	}

163	   163	      {!list.length && <p>No opening lessons are available in this work.</p>}
164	   164	    </div>}

['foundation'] : []), ...(steps.some(step => step.unitId === 'group-1') ? ['group-1'] : []), ...(steps.some(step => step.unitId === 'group-2') ? ['group-2'] : [])]}/>}

167	   167	    {current && (current.type === 'note' || run) && <div className={current.type === 'note' ? 'oc-course-note' : 'oc-drill-run-header'}>

169	   169	        <h1 className="font-mc-serif text-ink leading-[0.94] italic text-[clamp(3.1rem,7vw,6rem)] font-medium tracking-[-0.06em]">{current.title}</h1>
170	   170	        <p className="font-mc-sans text-body-prose mt-4 text-ink-soft max-w-[60ch] leading-relaxed">{current.id === 'necessary' ? <strong>{current.description}</strong> : current.description}</p>
171	   171	      </header> : <header><p className="oc-label">Practice</p><h1>{current.title}</h1></header>}</div>
172	   172	      {current.type === 'drill' && <Link className="oc-back oc-drill-back" to={url(view)}>Back to instructions</Link>}
173	   173	    </div>}
174	   174	    <div className="oc-course-bodies">{steps.filter(step => visited.includes(step.id)).map(step => <div key={step.id} hidden={step.id !== view || (step.type === 'drill' && !run)} className={step.id === 'foundation-practice' ? 'oc-course-body d1-body' : 'oc-course-body co-opening cr-sequence'}><CourseFlashcardUrl.Provider value={url}>{getSourceWorksheetDrill(step.id) ? <SourceWorksheetDrill setId={step.id} run startUrl={url(step.id, true)} storageScope={storageScope}/> : <OpeningCourseContent id={step.id}/>}</CourseFlashcardUrl.Provider></div>)}</div>
175	   175	    {current?.type === 'note' && <LessonCompletion key={current.id} stepId={current.id}/>}
176	   176	    {current && (current.type === 'note' || run) && <nav className="oc-course-turn" aria-label="Course navigation">{prior ? <Link to={url(prior.id)}><span>Previous {prior.type === 'note' ? 'lesson' : 'drill'}</span><strong>{prior.title}</strong></Link> : <Link to={url('lessons')}>All lessons</Link>}{next ? <Link to={url(next.id)}><span>Next {next.type === 'note' ? 'lesson' : 'drill'}</span><strong>{next.title}<ArrowRight size={18}/></strong></Link> : <Link to={url('lessons')}>Return to lessons<ArrowRight size={18}/></Link>}</nav>}
177	   177	    {!isIndex && view !== 'tools' && !current && <div className="oc-course-note"><h1>This lesson is not available here.</h1><Link to={url('lessons')}>Return to lessons</Link></div>}

180	   180	import { ContrapositiveLecture, DoubleNegativeDemo, FlipAndNegateDemo, Neg, PopQuizTitle, PulseWord, PunchWord, RuleDrill, ThreeMoves } from './ContrapositiveVisuals';

183	   183	import { conditionalReferenceContent, ReferenceQuestion, RuleDiagram, TargetReader, targetExamples, WordFamily } from './ConditionalReferenceSequence';

191	   191	type ContentSection = ReactElement<{ id?: string; number?: number; title?: string; children?: ReactNode; dark?: boolean; 'data-reference-boundary'?: boolean }>;
192	   192	
193	   193	// Compose the approved source sections directly. Their stateful exercises are
194	   194	// mounted by React; the former reader-page renderer is never invoked here.
195	   195	function sections(children: ReactNode): ContentSection[] {
196	   196	  return Children.toArray(children).flatMap(child => {
197	   197	    if (!isValidElement<ContentSection['props']>(child)) return [];
198	   198	    return child.type === Fragment ? sections(child.props.children) : [child];
199	   199	  });
200	   200	}
201	   201	
202	   202	function reference(number: number): ContentSection {
203	   203	  const section = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(item => item.props.number === number);
204	   204	  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional reference section ${number}`);
205	   205	  return section;
206	   206	}
207	   207	
208	   208	function groupSections(group: 'group-1' | 'group-2') {
209	   209	  return sections(GroupLessons({ id: group }).props.children);
210	   210	}
211	   211	
212	   212	function groupSection(group: 'group-1' | 'group-2', title: string) {
213	   213	  const section = groupSections(group).find(item => item.props.id === title || item.props.title === title);
214	   214	  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional section: ${title}`);
215	   215	  const sectionId = `course-${group}-${title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-')}`;
216	   216	  return cloneElement(section, { key: sectionId, id: sectionId });
217	   217	}
218	   218	
219	   219	function NoteSection({ title, children }: { title: string; children: ReactNode }) {
220	   220	  return <section className="co-section"><div className="co-section-heading"><h2>{title}</h2></div>{children}</section>;
221	   221	}
222	   222	
223	   223	function ReferenceBody({ number, omitTransition = false }: { number: number; omitTransition?: boolean }) {
224	   224	  const source = reference(number);
225	   225	  const children = Children.toArray(source.props.children);
226	   226	  // These source sections end in navigation prose for the former ordering.
227	   227	  // The course shell now owns transitions; the teaching stays in its source.
228	   228	  return <section className={`co-section cr-course-section ${source.props.dark ? 'cr-dark' : ''}`} data-reference-source={number}>
229	   229	    <div className="co-section-heading"><h2>{source.props.title}</h2></div>
230	   230	    <div className="cr-step-body">{omitTransition ? children.slice(0, -1) : children}</div>
231	   231	  </section>;
232	   232	}

234	   234	function FamilySupplement({ group }: { group: 'group-1' | 'group-2' }) {
235	   235	  const source = reference(group === 'group-1' ? 3 : 5);
236	   236	  const family = sections(source.props.children).find(child => child.type === WordFamily);
237	   237	  if (!family) throw new Error(`Missing approved ${group} supplementary family`);
238	   238	  return <details className="co-practical-note"><summary>More indicators for the same rule</summary>
239	   239	    <p>Use the stated rule: anyone who borrows a locker has a key. These rules keep that connection. They do not say every person with a key borrows a locker.</p>
240	   240	    {family}
241	   241	  </details>;
242	   242	}
243	   243	
244	   244	// Only and only if both mark the requirement, from different places in the rule.
245	   245	function OnlyAndOnlyIf() {
246	   246	  return <NoteSection title="Only and only if can mark the same requirement">
247	   247	    <div className="cr-language-pair"><p><strong>Only</strong> [people with a key] borrow a locker.</p><p>A person borrows a locker <strong>only if</strong> [they have a key].</p><span>Only and only if identify the necessary condition here.</span></div>
248	   248	    <p>Both sentences say locker borrowers have a key. In the first, only is at the beginning. In the second, only if is in the middle. Having a key is required in both, so it goes right. Read only if as the whole phrase; the if inside it isn't doing the job if does on its own.</p>
249	   249	    <ReferenceQuestion prompt="A person borrows a locker only if they have a key. Which condition goes on the right?" options={['Borrows a locker', 'Has a key']} correct={1} feedback={['Borrowing is what has the requirement. Ask what a locker borrower must have: a key. Borrowing goes left; having a key goes right.', 'Having a key is required for borrowing. Only if appears in the middle, but it still identifies the necessary condition.']}/>
250	   250	  </NoteSection>;
251	   251	}
252	   252	
253	   253	// Only after is tested in Drills 2 and 5, so it is taught with the other Group 2 words.
254	   254	function OnlyAfter() {
255	   255	  return <NoteSection title="Only after also marks a requirement">
256	   256	    <blockquote>The pharmacy fills a prescription <strong>only after</strong> a doctor signs it.</blockquote>
257	   257	    <p>Use the What Test: ask what the indicator refers to. <strong>Only after what?</strong> A doctor signs it. That signature is required before the pharmacy fills the prescription, so it goes right.</p>
258	   258	    <RuleDiagram left="Pharmacy fills the prescription" right="A doctor has signed it"/>
259	   259	    <p>Read it as: if the pharmacy fills a prescription, a doctor has signed it. The word after tells you the order of events in the rule. The arrow still reads as must, not as a timeline.</p>
260	   260	    <ReferenceQuestion prompt="A doctor has signed Ana's prescription. Does this rule prove the pharmacy fills it?" options={['Yes. The signature is enough.', 'No. Whether it gets filled is still open.']} correct={1} feedback={['The signature is what filling requires. Could a doctor sign a prescription that Ana never brings to the pharmacy? The rule would allow that.', 'The signature is required, not enough. The rule tells you what every filled prescription has. It does not say every signed one gets filled.']}/>
261	   261	  </NoteSection>;
262	   262	}
263	   263	
264	   264	// Follows the Group 1 indicator note, whose if, all and any sections already
265	   265	// teach the report, registered-guest and late-applicant sentences.
266	   266	function CompleteConditionNote() {
267	   267	  return <NoteSection title="Keep the whole phrase before you place it">
268	   268	    <blockquote><strong>The only people admitted</strong> are members.</blockquote>
269	   269	    <p>The only what? People admitted. An admitted person must be a member, so <strong>Person admitted → Person is a member</strong>. Keep the whole phrase together; the rule does not promise admission to every member.</p>
270	   270	    <ReferenceQuestion prompt="The only notebooks kept on this shelf are hardback. Which condition is sufficient?" options={['Notebook is hardback', 'Notebook is kept on this shelf', 'Notebook']} correct={1} feedback={['Being hardback does not guarantee a place on this shelf. The rule tells us about the notebooks kept here.', 'A notebook kept on this shelf must be hardback. Kept on this shelf is the complete sufficient condition.', 'Notebook alone would cover every notebook. Keep the restriction: kept on this shelf.']}/>
271	   271	    <p>In the next drill, find the indicator, select the complete condition, place it beside the arrow and check that your diagram keeps the rule's meaning.</p>
272	   272	  </NoteSection>;
273	   273	}
274	   274	
275	   275	function FourRules() {
276	   276	  return <section className="co-section"><div className="co-section-heading"><PopQuizTitle/></div><RuleDrill/></section>;
277	   277	}
278	   278	
279	   279	function ContrapositiveNote() {
280	   280	  return <>
281	   281	    <NoteSection title="What a contrapositive is">
282	   282	      <p>If the sufficient condition happens, the necessary condition <strong>must</strong> happen. The left side of the arrow guarantees the right side.</p>
283	   283	      <p>The contrapositive is what you know when the right side of the arrow goes missing:</p>
284	   284	      <p className="ct-definition"><strong>If the <span className="ct-hl">necessary condition</span> is absent, the <span className="ct-hl" data-tone="amber">sufficient condition</span> is absent too.</strong></p>
285	   285	      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the ceiling light is on, then the power must be on.</strong> With no power, the light can't be on. No power, no light. As a rule: <strong>If the power is off, then the ceiling light must be off.</strong></p>
286	   286	    </NoteSection>
287	   287	    <NoteSection title="You already knew this">
288	   288	      <p>The contrapositive was inside the original rule the whole time, waiting for you to spot it. That's why the two are <PulseWord>logically equivalent</PulseWord>: if one is true, the other is true, and you can use either. They're <PunchWord>interchangeable!</PunchWord></p>
289	   289	    </NoteSection>
290	   290	    <NoteSection title="Watch the lesson">
291	   291	      <ContrapositiveLecture/>
292	   292	    </NoteSection>
293	   293	    <NoteSection title="How to write it">
294	   294	      <p>Write the rule with an arrow: <strong>Light → Power</strong>. The sufficient condition goes on the left side of the arrow. The necessary condition goes on the right side of the arrow.</p>
295	   295	      <p>The contrapositive is <strong><Neg>Power</Neg> → <Neg>Light</Neg></strong>. A line through an idea means “not.” You get there in two steps:</p>
296	   296	      <ol className="ct-moves">
297	   297	        <li><strong>Step 1: Flip the sides.</strong> The right side goes on the left, and the left side goes on the right.</li>
298	   298	        <li><strong>Step 2: Negate both.</strong> Put “not” on each idea. If an idea already has a “not,” the two cancel: a double negative turns positive.</li>
299	   299	      </ol>
300	   300	      <FlipAndNegateDemo/>
301	   301	      <p>Same two steps when a side already has a “not.” Watch what happens to it:</p>
302	   302	      <DoubleNegativeDemo/>
303	   303	    </NoteSection>
304	   304	    <NoteSection title="What it is not">
305	   305	      <p>Two look-alikes fail. Wrong answer choices use them on purpose.</p>
306	   306	      <ThreeMoves/>
307	   307	    </NoteSection>
308	   308	    <NoteSection title="Read it, don't always write it">
309	   309	      <p>You can read a rule backward without writing anything: go from right to left and put a “not” on everything. Write the contrapositive when it helps you see the rule or connect it to another rule.</p>
310	   310	    </NoteSection>
311	   311	  </>;
312	   312	}
313	   313	
314	   314	function IndicatorNote({ group }: { group: 'group-1' | 'group-2' }) {
315	   315	  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);
316	   316	  const [opened, setOpened] = useState<number[]>([0]);
317	   317	  const [focusPanel, setFocusPanel] = useState(false);
318	   318	  const panels = useRef<(HTMLDivElement | null)[]>([]);
319	   319	  const id = useId();
320	   320	  const content = groupSections(group);
321	   321	  const expressions = content.slice(2, 9);
322	   322	  const names = expressions.map(section => section.props.title?.split(':')[0]);
323	   323	  useEffect(() => {
324	   324	    if (!focusPanel) return;
325	   325	    panels.current[selected]?.focus();
326	   326	    setFocusPanel(false);
327	   327	  }, [focusPanel, selected]);
328	   328	  const choose = (index: number, moveFocus = false) => {
329	   329	    setSelected(index);
330	   330	    setOpened(previous => previous.includes(index) ? previous : [...previous, index]);
331	   331	    setFocusPanel(moveFocus);
332	   332	  };
333	   333	  const last = selected === expressions.length - 1;
334	   334	  // Two introductions and the seven complete expression explanations belong
335	   335	  // together in one concept note, not nine separate navigation destinations.
336	   336	  return <>{content.slice(0, 2)}
337	   337	    <p className="co-word-guide">Work through all seven indicators. Each one does the same job in a different sentence, and each has its own trap to check. Use Next indicator to go in order, or choose any indicator to read it again.</p>
338	   338	    <div className="co-word-tabs" role="group" aria-label="Choose an indicator">{expressions.map((section, index) => <button type="button" key={section.props.title} aria-pressed={selected === index} aria-controls={`${id}-${index}`} onClick={() => choose(index)}>{names[index]}{opened.includes(index) && <><span aria-hidden="true"> ✓</span><span className="co-sr-only">, opened</span></>}</button>)}</div>
339	   339	    <p className="co-kicker" role="status">Indicator {selected + 1} of {expressions.length} · {opened.length} of {expressions.length} opened</p>
340	   340	    {expressions.map((section, index) => <div key={section.props.title} id={`${id}-${index}`} hidden={selected !== index} tabIndex={-1} ref={element => { panels.current[index] = element; }}>{section}</div>)}
341	   341	    <div className="co-choices">{last
342	   342	      ? <p>That was the last of the seven indicators. Choose any of them to read it again, or recall them with the cards below.</p>
343	   343	      : <button type="button" className="co-primary" onClick={() => choose(selected + 1, true)}>Next indicator: {names[selected + 1]}</button>}</div>
344	   344	    {group === 'group-2' && <><OnlyAndOnlyIf/><OnlyAfter/></>}
345	   345	    <FamilySupplement group={group}/>
346	   346	  </>;
347	   347	}
348	   348	
349	   349	// Teach placement and reading through the familiar relationship, then require a decision.
350	   350	function MustArrowNote() {
351	   351	  return <section className="co-section cr-course-section ma-lesson" data-reference-source={8}>
352	   352	    <div className="co-section-heading"><h2>What the arrow says</h2></div>
353	   353	    <div className="cr-step-body">
354	   354	      <p className="ma-lead">An arrow joins the two conditions in a rule. It says one thing: <strong>if the condition on the left is true, the condition on the right must be true.</strong></p>
355	   355	      <figure className="ma-example" aria-label="If you are enrolled in law school, you must be a student">
356	   356	        <figcaption className="ma-read-instruction">Read it as a sentence: <strong>“If you are enrolled in law school, then you must be a student.”</strong></figcaption>
357	   357	        <div className="ma-relationship">
358	   358	          <div className="ma-condition"><span className="ma-side"><small>Left side</small>Sufficient condition</span><strong>You are enrolled in law school</strong><span className="ma-meaning">Enough to guarantee the right side</span></div>
359	   359	          <div className="ma-arrow" aria-hidden="true"><span>must</span><svg viewBox="0 0 100 32" fill="none"><path d="M3 16H93M77 3L94 16L77 29" stroke="currentColor" strokeWidth="5" strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round"/></svg></div>
360	   360	          <div className="ma-condition"><span className="ma-side"><small>Right side</small>Necessary condition</span><strong>You are a student</strong><span className="ma-meaning">Required when the left side is true</span></div>
361	   361	        </div>
362	   362	      </figure>
363	   363	      <p>Being enrolled in law school is <strong>enough</strong> to know you are a student. That is why enrollment goes on the <strong>left</strong>. Being a student is <strong>required</strong> for enrollment, so it goes on the <strong>right</strong>. The arrow points from what is enough to what is required.</p>
364	   364	      <section className="ma-where" aria-label="Where to start reading">
365	   365	        <h3>Start at the left</h3>
366	   366	        <p>A rule begins where the arrow begins. Say “if” and read the left condition. Then say “then” and read the right one: <strong>If you are enrolled in law school, then you are a student.</strong></p>
367	   367	        <p>Reading from the right says something else. “You are a student, so you are enrolled” is not what this rule says. The arrow only runs one way, and you start at its tail.</p>
368	   368	      </section>
369	   369	      <section className="ma-purpose" aria-label="What the arrow is for">
370	   370	        <h3>What the arrow is for</h3>
371	   371	        <p>A rule can take a full sentence to say. The arrow shows what it promises in one line, so you can test a fact against it without rereading the sentence.</p>
372	   372	        <ul>
373	   373	          <li>You learn the <strong>left</strong> side is true. The right side must be true too.</li>
374	   374	          <li>You learn the <strong>right</strong> side is true. The left side stays open.</li>
375	   375	        </ul>
376	   376	        <p>This matters on the LSAT because arguments turn on which way a rule runs. A conclusion that follows the arrow is supported. A conclusion that runs against it needs something the rule never gave you.</p>
377	   377	      </section>
378	   378	      <section className="ma-placement" aria-label="Place and read the conditions">
379	   379	        <h3>Place the conditions. Then read the arrow.</h3>
380	   380	        <p>Choose what belongs on each side, then use the same direction with a new rule.</p>
381	   381	        <p className="ma-compact-note">Put the <strong>condition</strong> beside the arrow, not the indicator word. For example, write “you win the raffle,” not “if.”</p>
382	   382	        <MustArrowPractice />
383	   383	      </section>
384	   384	      <section className="ma-order" aria-label="Two ways to say the same thing">
385	   385	        <h3>Two ways to say the same thing</h3>
386	   386	        <div className="cr-language-pair"><p><strong>If</strong> you win the raffle, you get a bicycle.</p><p>You get a bicycle <strong>if</strong> you win the raffle.</p></div>
387	   387	        <p>Both mean: <strong>You win the raffle → You get a bicycle.</strong> Winning is sufficient, so it goes left, even when it comes last in the rule.</p>
388	   388	      </section>
389	   389	      <p className="ma-takeaway"><strong>The arrow guarantees the right side when the left side is true.</strong> Being a student alone does not prove that you are enrolled in law school.</p>
390	   390	      <details className="cr-extension ma-reverse"><summary>Why can't we reverse it?</summary><div className="ma-reverse-body"><p>A person could be a history student who is not enrolled in law school. That possibility fits our rule.</p><p>The arrow states a rule. By itself, it does not tell us that either condition is true, or that one causes the other.</p></div></details>
391	   391	    </div>
392	   392	  </section>;
393	   393	}

395	   395	// One translation process, checked four ways, instead of five restarted lessons.
396	   396	function WholeMeaningNote() {
397	   397	  const onlyComparison = groupSection('group-2', 'Compare only with the only');
398	   398	  const insideCheck = Children.toArray(reference(12).props.children);
399	   399	  return <>
400	   400	    <NoteSection title="Check a translation four ways">
401	   401	      <p>A diagram can point the right way and still say the wrong thing. Each check below catches a different slip in the same translation process.</p>
402	   402	      <ol>
403	   403	        <li><strong>Target:</strong> did you find the whole idea the indicator refers to, wherever it sits?</li>
404	   404	        <li><strong>Qualification:</strong> did your short label keep every restriction?</li>
405	   405	        <li><strong>Direction:</strong> did a similar-looking indicator trick you into the wrong side?</li>
406	   406	        <li><strong>Imagined case:</strong> does your arrow promise something the rule leaves open?</li>
407	   407	      </ol>
408	   408	    </NoteSection>
409	   409	    <NoteSection title="Check 1: find the whole group, wherever it sits">
410	   410	      <p>An indicator's <strong>target</strong> is the complete word or phrase it refers to. In “All guests wearing blue badges receive vouchers,” all refers to guests wearing blue badges, not guests alone.</p>
411	   411	      <p>The group often comes after its indicator. But often isn't always. Try these rules in order.</p>
412	   412	      {targetExamples.map(item => <TargetReader item={item} key={item.label}/>)}
413	   413	      <div className="cr-language-pair"><p>[Having a key] is <strong>required</strong> for borrowing a locker.</p><p>Borrowing a locker <strong>requires</strong> [having a key].</p><span>Having a key is the requirement in both sentences.</span></div>
414	   414	      <p>The group comes before required in the first sentence and after requires in the second. Having a key still goes right. Neither the closest noun nor the next word is a reliable group rule. Ask which complete idea the rule calls required, then give that idea its role.</p>
415	   415	    </NoteSection>
416	   416	    <NoteSection title="Check 2: keep every qualification">
417	   417	      <p>Once you have the group, shorten it without losing what limits it. These two sentences test the same habit from opposite sides of the arrow.</p>
418	   418	      <div className="co-word-comparison">
419	   419	        <div><blockquote>Any applicant who submits late pays a fee.</blockquote><p>Applicant alone isn't the condition. The rule is about an applicant who submits late. Drop that restriction and your diagram makes a claim about on-time applicants too.</p></div>
420	   420	        <div><blockquote>Entry requires a current pass.</blockquote><p>A current pass is required. Has a pass drops current and would include an expired pass. A shorter label should save writing, not change the requirement.</p></div>
421	   421	      </div>
422	   422	      <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Any applicant who submits late pays a fee. What goes left?" options={['Applicant', 'Applicant submits late']} correct={1} feedback={['You dropped the late-submission restriction. Applicant alone would cover every applicant, including someone who submits on time. The sufficient idea is applicant submits late.', 'This keeps the complete sufficient idea. The rule covers applicants who submit late, so keep that restriction with the applicant.']}/>
423	   423	      <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Entry requires a current pass. What goes right?" options={['Has current pass', 'Has any pass']} correct={0} feedback={['This keeps the full requirement. Entry needs a current pass; an expired pass does not meet that requirement.', 'You dropped current from the requirement. Has any pass would include an expired pass, which is not what the rule requires.']}/>
424	   424	    </NoteSection>
425	   425	    <NoteSection title="Check 3: only and the only point to different groups">{onlyComparison.props.children}</NoteSection>
426	   426	    <NoteSection title="Can, should and must keep their meaning">
427	   427	      <blockquote>If a visitor registers, the visitor may enter.</blockquote>
428	   428	      <p>Registration guarantees <strong>permission to enter</strong>. It does not mean the visitor actually enters. Keep “may enter” together on the right.</p>
429	   429	      <blockquote>If a report contains an error, the editor should correct it.</blockquote>
430	   430	      <p>The rule tells us what the editor <strong>should do</strong>. It does not prove that the correction happens. A diagram must keep the obligation instead of turning it into a fact.</p>
431	   431	      <p>Keep time limits too. <strong>“Before noon”</strong> is not the same as “at some point.” When two ideas form one condition, keep both: <strong>“signed and dated”</strong> is the complete requirement.</p>
432	   432	    </NoteSection>
433	   433	    <NoteSection title="Short symbols stand for complete conditions">
434	   434	      <p>You choose the letters. Write what each one means so a short diagram keeps the whole rule.</p>
435	   435	      <blockquote>All registered guests receive a wristband.</blockquote>
436	   436	      <p><strong>R</strong> = guest is registered. <strong>W</strong> = guest receives a wristband.</p>
437	   437	      <RuleDiagram left="R" right="W"/>
438	   438	      <p>Read it back: <strong>If a guest is registered, the guest receives a wristband.</strong> R does not mean “guest” alone. Its definition keeps “registered.”</p>
439	   439	    </NoteSection>
440	   440	    <NoteSection title="Check 4: try the rule from the inside">{insideCheck.slice(0, -1)}</NoteSection>
441	   441	  </>;
442	   442	}
443	   443	
444	   444	const freshCases = [
445	   445	  { rule: 'If it rains on Saturday, the market moves into the town hall.', questions: [
446	   446	    { prompt: "It's Friday. You've read this rule and nothing else. Where will the market be on Saturday?", options: ['In the town hall.', 'Outdoors, as usual.', "The rule alone doesn't settle it."], correct: 2, feedback: ['You treated the rule as a weather report. It tells you what follows if it rains. Nobody has told you that it will rain.', "You assumed it won't rain. The rule hasn't told you that either, so the market's location is still open.", "The rule describes what would happen if it rains. It's a promise about a possible case, not a report that the case happened."] },
447	   447	    { prompt: "Saturday arrives, and it's raining. Now where is the market?", options: ['In the town hall.', 'Still not settled.'], correct: 0, feedback: ['Now you have a fact, not just a rule. Rain on Saturday meets the condition, so the rule puts the market in the town hall.', "The fact changes things. The rule covers a rainy Saturday, and that's what we have, so the market's location is settled."] },
448	   448	  ] },
449	   449	  { rule: 'Every student going on the museum trip has a signed permission slip.', questions: [
450	   450	    { prompt: 'Which description matches this rule?', options: ['Going on the trip is enough to know a student has a signed slip.', 'Having a signed slip is enough to know a student is going on the trip.', 'Each one is enough to know the other.'], correct: 0, feedback: ['Pick any student on the trip: the rule says they have a signed slip. Going is enough to prove the slip, and the slip is what every student on the trip is required to have.', "You made the requirement enough. A student could have a signed slip and stay at school with a cold. That student doesn't break the rule.", "The rule gives only one direction. Going on the trip proves a signed slip. A student with a slip who stays behind fits the rule, so the slip doesn't prove going."] },
451	   451	  ] },
452	   452	  { rule: 'If the café runs out of bread, it closes early.', questions: [
453	   453	    { prompt: 'Today the café did not run out of bread. Did it close early?', options: ['No. It stayed open until the usual time.', 'Yes. It closed early.', 'Not settled. It could stay open or close early for another reason.'], correct: 2, feedback: ['You treated running out of bread as the only reason to close early. The rule gives one reason. A café that closed early because of a power cut would still fit it.', "Nothing here proves an early closing. The rule tells us what happens when the bread runs out, and today it didn't.", "Given that fact, the rule doesn't settle whether the café closed early. It could have stayed open, or closed early for another reason. Both cases fit the rule."] },
454	   454	  ] },

457	   457	// Recall the definitions, apply the arrow, then distinguish indicators from conditions.
458	   458	const foundationConceptQuestions: DrillOneQuestion[] = [
459	   459	  { contextLabel: 'Concept', rule: 'Sufficient and necessary conditions', prompt: 'What is a necessary condition?', options: ['A requirement that must be met, but might not be enough on its own.', 'Something that always guarantees the other condition.', 'Something that usually happens afterward.'], correct: 0, feedback: ['Necessary means required. Meeting the requirement does not, by itself, prove that the other condition is true.', 'That describes sufficiency. A requirement can also be sufficient, but being necessary alone does not prove that.', 'Necessity is a requirement, not a prediction about timing. It must hold whenever the condition that requires it holds.'] },
460	   460	  { contextLabel: 'Concept', rule: 'Sufficient and necessary conditions', prompt: 'What is a sufficient condition?', options: ['The only possible way for another condition to be true.', 'Something that makes another condition more likely.', 'Something that guarantees another condition when it is true.'], correct: 2, feedback: ['The only possible way describes a necessary condition: it would be required. Sufficient means enough, without claiming it is the only way.', 'A conditional guarantee is stronger than likelihood. The other condition must be true whenever the sufficient condition is true.', 'Sufficient means enough. Once it is true, the rule guarantees the necessary condition.'] },
461	   461	  { contextLabel: 'Diagram', rule: 'A → B', prompt: 'Which condition belongs on the left side of this arrow?', options: ['The necessary condition.', 'The sufficient condition.', 'Whichever condition appears first in the rule.'], correct: 1, feedback: ['The necessary condition is the one on the right: what must be true. Which role does the condition on the left play?', 'A is the sufficient condition. If A is true, B must be true.', 'Sentence position can change. Place the sufficient condition on the left even if it appears last in the rule.'] },
462	   462	  { contextLabel: 'Diagram', rule: 'A → B', prompt: 'What does the condition on the right side represent?', options: ['A fact that has already happened.', 'A condition that guarantees A.', 'A requirement that must hold whenever A holds.'], correct: 2, feedback: ['The arrow alone does not tell us that B is actually true. It tells us B must be true if A is true.', 'This reverses the arrow. A guarantees B; the rule does not say B guarantees A.', 'B is necessary for A. A cannot be true while B is false.'] },
463	   463	  { contextLabel: 'Diagram', rule: 'A → B', prompt: 'How do you read this arrow?', options: ['If A is true, B must be true.', 'If B is true, A must be true.', 'A happens first and causes B to happen.'], correct: 0, feedback: ['Read from left to right. The truth of A guarantees the truth of B.', 'That reads against the arrow. B might be true without A being true.', 'The arrow gives a guarantee, not a timeline or a cause. A and B may be true at the same time.'], conditions: ['A', 'B'] },
464	   464	  { rule: 'If a painting is in the permanent collection, it has an inventory number.', prompt: 'The sufficient condition is the one that is enough: when it is true, the other condition must be true. It does not have to cause the other condition or happen first. Which complete condition is sufficient here?', options: ['The painting has an inventory number.', 'The painting is in the permanent collection.', 'The word “if.”'], correct: 1, feedback: ['An inventory number is what the rule says a painting must have. That makes it the necessary condition. Look for the condition that is enough to prove it.', 'Being in the permanent collection is enough to prove an inventory number. Sufficient describes that logical role, not a cause or an earlier event.', 'If is an indicator, not a condition. Look for the complete idea it introduces.'], conditions: ['Painting is in the permanent collection', 'Painting has an inventory number'] },
465	   465	  { rule: 'If a book is on the reserved shelf, it has a reservation card.', prompt: 'The necessary condition is what must be true when the sufficient condition holds. Which condition is necessary here?', options: ['The book has a reservation card.', 'The book is on the reserved shelf.', 'Someone places the book on the shelf later.'], correct: 0, feedback: ['The reservation card is necessary. A book on that shelf must have one. Necessary here does not mean an event that happens later.', 'Being on the reserved shelf is what if introduces, so it is the condition that is enough. Look for what the rule says a book on that shelf must have.', 'The rule says nothing about a later action. Look for what the rule says every book on the reserved shelf already has.'], conditions: ['Book is on the reserved shelf', 'Book has a reservation card'] },
466	   466	  { rule: 'If a parcel is marked fragile, it is packed with padding.', prompt: 'Which complete condition goes on the left?', options: ['If.', 'The parcel is packed with padding.', 'The parcel is marked fragile.'], correct: 2, feedback: ['If helps identify the condition, but is not itself a condition. Write the complete idea it introduces.', 'Padding is required for a parcel marked fragile, so it goes on the right.', 'Being marked fragile guarantees padding under this rule. That complete condition goes on the left.'], conditions: ['Parcel is marked fragile', 'Parcel is packed with padding'] },
467	   467	  { rule: 'If an application is approved, it has a reference number.', prompt: 'Which complete condition goes on the right?', options: ['The application is approved.', 'The application has a reference number.', 'The word “if.”'], correct: 1, feedback: ['Approval is what if introduces. That makes it the condition that is enough, and the right side holds what it requires.', 'Having a reference number is required for an approved application. It goes on the right.', 'If is an indicator, not a condition. Put a complete idea beside the arrow.'], conditions: ['Application is approved', 'Application has a reference number'] },
468	   468	  { rule: 'A visitor receives a badge if the visitor signs in.', prompt: 'Which diagram keeps this rule?', options: ['Visitor receives a badge → Visitor signs in', 'Visitor signs in → Visitor receives a badge', 'Both directions are guaranteed.'], correct: 1, feedback: ['This reverses the guarantee. The rule gives a badge to someone who signs in, not a sign-in to everyone with a badge.', 'If introduces signing in, even at the end of the rule. Signing in is sufficient and goes left.', 'The rule guarantees only one direction. It does not exclude a visitor who receives a badge another way.'], conditions: ['Visitor signs in', 'Visitor receives a badge'] },
469	   469	  { rule: 'If a member books a room, the member must have an active account.', prompt: 'Which word identifies the sufficient condition here?', options: ['If.', 'Must.', 'Both words identify the sufficient condition.'], correct: 0, feedback: ['If introduces the sufficient condition: the member books a room. Must expresses the requirement that follows from it.', 'Must tells us what is required: having an active account. Look for the word that introduces the condition that is enough.', 'The two words have different jobs. Only one of them introduces what is enough; the other marks what is required.'], conditions: ['Member books a room', 'Member has an active account'] },
470	   470	  { rule: 'Every volunteer wearing a green badge must have completed training.', prompt: 'Which word acts as the sufficient-condition indicator?', options: ['Must.', 'Wearing.', 'Every.'], correct: 2, feedback: ['Must marks what is required: completed training. An indicator is the word that tells you the rule applies to that whole group.', 'Every covers all volunteers wearing a green badge. That qualified group is sufficient to prove the training that must have been completed.'], conditions: ['Volunteer wears a green badge', 'Volunteer has completed training'] },
471	   471	  { rule: 'Every parcel weighing more than ten kilograms has a handling label.', prompt: 'Which complete condition belongs on the left?', options: ['The item is a parcel.', 'The item is a parcel weighing more than ten kilograms.', 'The item has a handling label.'], correct: 1, feedback: ['Parcel alone drops the weight restriction. The rule does not promise a handling label for every parcel.', 'Keep the weight restriction. The rule guarantees a handling label for every parcel weighing more than ten kilograms.', 'Having a handling label is the required condition, so it goes on the right.'], conditions: ['Parcel weighs more than ten kilograms', 'Parcel has a handling label'] },
472	   472	  { rule: 'A document is archived only if it has a catalogue number.', prompt: 'Which indicator introduces the requirement?', options: ['Only if.', 'If, taken on its own.', 'Archived.'], correct: 0, feedback: ['Read only if as one phrase. 473	   473	  { rule: 'A researcher enters the reading room only if the researcher has a visitor permit.', prompt: 'Which diagram matches this rule?', options: ['Has a visitor permit → Enters the reading room', 'Only if → Has a visitor permit', 'Enters the reading room → Has a visitor permit'], correct: 2, feedback: ['This arrow starts from having a permit. 474	   474	  { rule: 'Filing a claim requires a signed form.', prompt: 'What is the necessary condition?', options: ['Filing a claim.', 'Having a signed form.', 'Having any form, even an unsigned one.'], correct: 1, feedback: ['Filing is what has the requirement. 478	   478	/**
479	   479	 * Spot the condition saves answers by position. Order 1 put the concept and
480	   480	 * indicator questions first and the rule-vs-fact questions last; order 2 puts
481	   481	 * the rule-vs-fact questions first. Map each current position to its order 1
482	   482	 * position so earlier answers stay with the question they answered.
483	   483	 */
484	   484	const FOUNDATION_ORDER = 2;
485	   485	function foundationLegacyOrder(conceptCount: number, ruleCount: number): Record<number, number[]> {
486	   486	  return { 1: [...Array.from({ length: ruleCount }, (_, index) => conceptCount + index), ...Array.from({ length: conceptCount }, (_, index) => index)] };
487	   487	}
488	   488	
489	   489	function FoundationPractice() {
490	   490	  const anchor = sections(reference(1).props.children).find(child => child.type === ReferenceQuestion);
491	   491	  if (!anchor) throw new Error('Missing approved raffle retrieval question');
492	   492	  const fresh = sections(Fundamentals().props.children).find(section => section.props.title === 'Enough does not mean the only way');
493	   493	  if (!fresh) throw new Error('Missing approved fresh sufficient-condition check');
494	   494	  const freshContent = Children.toArray(fresh.props.children);
495	   495	  const freshCheck = freshContent[freshContent.length - 1];
496	   496	  if (!isValidElement<DrillOneQuestion>(freshCheck)) throw new Error('Missing approved delivery question');
497	   497	  const anchorQuestion = anchor as ReactElement<DrillOneQuestion>;
498	   498	  const questions: DrillOneQuestion[] = [
499	   499	    { ...anchorQuestion.props, rule: 'If you win the raffle, you get a bicycle.' },
500	   500	    ...freshCases.flatMap(item => item.questions.map(question => ({ ...question, rule: item.rule }))),
501	   501	    { ...freshCheck.props, rule: 'Every delivery marked collected has a pickup receipt.', prompt: 'What is sufficient here?' },
502	   502	    { rule: 'If you win the raffle, you get a bicycle.', prompt: 'Someone claims that getting a bicycle proves you won the raffle. Which possible case fits our rule but disproves that claim?', options: ['You win and get a bicycle.', 'You do not win, but get a bicycle as a gift.', 'You win and never get a bicycle.'], correct: 1, feedback: ['This fits both the rule and the claim, so it cannot disprove the claim. We need a case where the rule holds but the claim does not.', 'The rule promises a bicycle to a winner. It does not exclude a bicycle received as a gift without winning, so this case fits the rule and disproves the claim.', 'Winning without getting a bicycle breaks the original rule. We need a case that keeps the rule true.'] },
503	   503	  ];
504	   504	  const conditions: Array<[string, string]> = [
505	   505	    ['You win the raffle', 'You get a bicycle'],
506	   506	    ['It rains on Saturday', 'The market moves into the town hall'],
507	   507	    ['It rains on Saturday', 'The market moves into the town hall'],
508	   508	    ['A student goes on the museum trip', 'That student has a signed permission slip'],
509	   509	    ['The café runs out of bread', 'The café closes early'],
510	   510	    ['A delivery is marked collected', 'That delivery has a pickup receipt'],
511	   511	    ['You win the raffle', 'You get a bicycle'],
512	   512	  ];
513	   513	  const ruleAndFact = questions.map((question, index) => ({ ...question, conditions: conditions[index] }));
514	   514	  // Order 2: rule-vs-fact questions, then the five concept checks, then indicators.
515	   515	  const foundationPracticeQuestions = [...ruleAndFact, ...foundationConceptQuestions];
516	   516	  return <DrillOneExperience key={foundationPracticeQuestions.length} questions={foundationPracticeQuestions} order={FOUNDATION_ORDER} legacyOrder={foundationLegacyOrder(foundationConceptQuestions.length, ruleAndFact.length)}/>;
517	   517	}
518	   518	
519	   519	function PermissionBoundary() {
520	   520	  const boundary = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(section => section.props['data-reference-boundary'] !== undefined);
521	   521	  if (!boundary) throw new Error('Missing approved permission and instruction boundary');
522	   522	  return <NoteSection title="Keep permission and instructions distinct"><p>A conditional can give permission or an instruction. Keep that strength instead of turning it into a report of what someone did.</p>{Children.toArray(boundary.props.children).slice(3)}</NoteSection>;
523	   523	}
524	   524	
525	   525	function Content({ id }: { id: string }) {
526	   526	  switch (id) {
527	   527	    case 'meaning': return <ReferenceBody number={1} omitTransition/>;
528	   528	    case 'sufficient': return <ReferenceBody number={2} omitTransition/>;
529	   529	    case 'necessary': return <NecessaryNote/>;
530	   530	    case 'must-arrow': return <MustArrowNote/>;
531	   531	    case 'sufficient-words': return <><IndicatorNote group="group-1"/><CompleteConditionNote/></>;
532	   532	    case 'necessary-words': return <><IndicatorNote group="group-2"/><PermissionBoundary/></>;
533	   533	    case 'whole-meaning': return <WholeMeaningNote/>;
534	   534	    case 'contrapositive': return <><ContrapositiveNote/><FourRules/></>;
535	   535	    case 'four-situations': return <><FourSituationsNote/><section className="co-section"><div className="co-section-heading"><PopQuizTitle/></div><FourSituationsQuiz/></section></>;
536	   536	    case 'foundation-practice': return <FoundationPractice/>;

/** Reviewed derivatives of Joshua's Group 1/2 worksheets. Original source text and hashes are retained.
 * NOT (...) negates the entire proposition, including qualifiers and compound predicates.
 * Sets before contraposition contain its reference data for later use; the UI must gate its display by taskKind.
 */
export type SourceWorksheetTaskKind = 'identify' | 'indicators' | 'forward' | 'roles' | 'rephrase' | 'contrapositive';

  id: string; sourceId: string; originalStatement: string; statement: string;

  source: { file: string; sha256: string; part: string; item: number };
}
export interface SourceWorksheetDrillSet {

  instructions: string; items: SourceWorksheetItem[];
}

// Later visits reuse source items without counting them as new worksheet questions.
const contrapositiveRevisits: Record<string, { sourceSetId: string; title: string; shortTitle: string }> = {
  'source-intro-diagrams-contrapositive': {
    sourceSetId: 'source-intro-diagrams',

  },

export const getSourceWorksheetDrill = (id: string): SourceWorksheetDrillSet | undefined => {
  const canonical = sourceWorksheetDrills.find((set) => set.id === id);
  if (canonical) return canonical;
  const revisit = contrapositiveRevisits[id];
  if (!revisit) return undefined;
  const source = sourceWorksheetDrills.find((set) => set.id === revisit.sourceSetId);
  if (!source) return undefined;
  return {
    ...source,
    id,
    title: revisit.title,
    shortTitle: revisit.shortTitle,
    stage: 'After contraposition',
    taskKind: 'contrapositive',
    instructions: 'Return to these familiar sentences. Find the indicators, write the complete conditions, and define your symbols. Build the original diagram and compare it with ours, then build the contrapositive and check it. Last, write both in ordinary if/then language. Reverse the conditions and negate each complete idea, including its restrictions.',
  };
};

/** A run of adjacent words the learner selected, with the group they chose ('1', '2' or ''). */
export interface IndicatorSelection { text: string; group: string }
export interface IndicatorCheck { correct: boolean; hint: string }

export const normalizeIndicator = (text: string) => text.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]/g, '').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();

// Accepted alternatives. Items keep one keyed indicator; these rules decide which other selections mean the same thing.
const DETERMINER_TAILS = ['those', 'the ones', 'these'];
const QUANTIFIERS = ['any', 'every', 'all', 'each', 'no', 'anyone', 'anything', 'everyone', 'everything', 'none'];
const WORD_ALTERNATIVES: Record<string, string[]> = { every: ['each and every'] };
const WHO_PHRASE = /^([a-z]+ )?who$/;

function acceptsSelection(expected: string, text: string) {
  if (text === expected || WORD_ALTERNATIVES[expected]?.includes(text)) return true;
  for (const tail of DETERMINER_TAILS) {
    // "Only those" was taught as "only": accept the head word, and the phrase continued by "who".
    if (expected.endsWith(` ${tail}`) && (text === expected.slice(0, -tail.length - 1) || text === `${expected} who`)) return true;
  }
  // A condition introduced by who: accept "who" or "[group] who" (e.g. "members who").
  if ((expected === 'who' || expected.endsWith(' who')) && WHO_PHRASE.test(text)) return true;
  // "Every applicant who" selected as one run still marks the quantifier.
  const words = text.split(' ');
  return QUANTIFIERS.includes(expected) && words.length <= 4 && words[0] === expected && words[words.length - 1] === 'who';
}

const containsRun = (outer: string, inner: string) => ` ${outer} `.includes(` ${inner} `);

/** Grades the indicator step and returns a hint that never names the keyed answer. */
export function checkIndicatorSelection(item: Pick<SourceWorksheetItem, 'statement' | 'indicators'>, selections: IndicatorSelection[]): IndicatorCheck {
  const expected = item.indicators.map(i => ({ text: normalizeIndicator(i.text), group: String(i.group) }));
  const remaining = [...expected];
  const statementWords = normalizeIndicator(item.statement).split(' ');
  const whoIntroducesCondition = statementWords.includes('who') && expected.some(e => e.group === '1' && QUANTIFIERS.includes(e.text));
  const extra: string[] = [];
  const wrongGroup: string[] = [];
  for (const selection of selections) {
    const text = normalizeIndicator(selection.text);
    const index = remaining.findIndex(e => acceptsSelection(e.text, text));
    if (index >= 0) {
      if (remaining[index].group !== selection.group) wrongGroup.push(selection.text);
      remaining.splice(index, 1);
      continue;
    }
    // Two adjacent indicators ("must always") form one run of selected words.
    const pairs: [number, number][] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < remaining.length; i++) for (let j = 0; j < remaining.length; j++) if (i !== j && `${remaining[i].text} ${remaining[j].text}` === text) pairs.push([i, j]);
    const pair = pairs[0];
    if (pair) {
      if (remaining[pair[0]].group !== selection.group || remaining[pair[1]].group !== selection.group) wrongGroup.push(selection.text);
      for (const i of [...pair].sort((a, b) => b - a)) remaining.splice(i, 1);
      continue;
    }
    if (whoIntroducesCondition && WHO_PHRASE.test(text)) {
      if (selection.group !== '1') wrongGroup.push(selection.text);
      continue;
    }
    extra.push(selection.text);
  }
  if (extra.length) {
    const text = extra[0];
    const normal = normalizeIndicator(text);
    if (expected.some(e => e.text !== normal && containsRun(e.text, normal))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is only part of an indicator. Select every word of the phrase.` };
    if (expected.some(e => containsRun(normal, e.text))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” includes words that are not part of the indicator. Select only the signal words.` };
    return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is not an indicator in this rule.` };
  }
  if (wrongGroup.length) return { correct: false, hint: `Check the group for “${wrongGroup[0]}”. Does it introduce what is enough, or what is required?` };
  if (remaining.length) return { correct: false, hint: remaining.length === 1 ? 'One indicator is still unselected. Read the whole rule again.' : `${remaining.length} indicators are still unselected. Read the whole rule again.` };
  return { correct: true, hint: '' };
}

/** Where each condition sits inside a rule, for the "highlight both conditions" step.
 * Keyed by the learner-facing statement. Each entry lists accepted wordings (separated by " | ") for the

 * The first wording in each list is the one shown on "Show me".
 */
const SPANS: Record<string, [sufficient: string, necessary: string]> = {

export type ConditionRole = 'sufficient' | 'necessary';
export type SpanCheck = { correct: boolean; roles: (ConditionRole | null)[]; hint: string };

const norm = (word: string) => word.replace(/’/g, "'").replace(/[^\p{L}\p{N}'-]/gu, '').toLowerCase();
const words = (text: string) => text.split(/\s+/).map(norm).filter(Boolean);
// A dropped article, modal or "to" at the edges is not a miss: the learner still found the condition.
const LEADING = new Set(['a', 'an', 'the', 'to', 'will', 'can', 'must', 'should', 'is', 'are']);
function core(list: string[]) {
  let start = 0;
  while (start < list.length - 1 && LEADING.has(list[start])) start += 1;
  let end = list.length;
  if (end - start > 1 && list[end - 1] === 'to') end -= 1;
  return list.slice(start, end).join(' ');
}
const accepted = (entry: string) => entry.split('|').map(text => core(words(text)));

export const hasConditionSpans = (statement: string) => statement in SPANS;
export function conditionAnswer(statement: string, role: ConditionRole): string {
  const entry = SPANS[statement]?.[role === 'sufficient' ? 0 : 1] ?? '';
  return entry.split('|')[0].trim();
}
/** Tokens match how the screen splits the rule: on whitespace, punctuation left attached. */
export function spanText(statement: string, span: number[]): string {
  const tokens = statement.split(/\s+/);
  return tokens.slice(span[0], span[1] + 1).join(' ').replace(/^[^\p{L}\p{N}]+/u, '').replace(/[.,;:!?]+$/, '');
}
export function roleOfSpan(statement: string, span: number[]): ConditionRole | null {
  const entry = SPANS[statement];
  if (!entry) return null;
  const tokens = statement.split(/\s+/);
  const picked = core(tokens.slice(span[0], span[1] + 1).map(norm).filter(Boolean));
  if (accepted(entry[0]).includes(picked)) return 'sufficient';
  if (accepted(entry[1]).includes(picked)) return 'necessary';
  return null;
}
export function checkConditionSpans(statement: string, spans: number[][]): SpanCheck {
  const roles = spans.map(span => roleOfSpan(statement, span));
  if (spans.length < 2) return { correct: false, roles, hint: 'Highlight both conditions. Click the first word of a condition, then its last word.' };
  if (roles.some(role => !role)) return { correct: false, roles, hint: 'One highlight is not a complete condition. Include every restriction, such as “within 30 days,” and leave the indicator out.' };
  if (roles[0] === roles[1]) return { correct: false, roles, hint: 'Both highlights are the same condition. The rule has two different ones.' };
  return { correct: true, roles, hint: '' };
}

/** Test hook: every authored wording must be found in its rule, without overlap. */
export function spanEntries() { return Object.entries(SPANS); }
export function locateWording(statement: string, wording: string): [number, number] | null {
  const tokens = statement.split(/\s+/).map(norm);
  const target = words(wording);
  for (let start = 0; start + target.length <= tokens.length; start += 1) {
    const slice = tokens.slice(start, start + target.length);
    if (slice.every((word, i) => word === target[i])) return [start, start + target.length - 1];
  }
  return null;
}

components/ConditionalReferenceSequence.tsx:320:      <details className="cr-extension"><summary>More Group 2 forms, with the rule intact</summary><p><strong>Only when</strong> and <strong>only where</strong> can restrict the occasions or places of an outcome: “The fountain runs only when its pump is on.” A running fountain requires an operating pump. The pump being on does not prove that the fountain runs.</p><p><strong>Requires</strong> directly names a requirement: “Entry requires a current pass.” Keep current. <strong>Always</strong> needs the rule's subject and scope: “The upstairs room is always quiet when the door is shut.” Here a shut door proves quiet, not the other way around.</p><p><strong>Only</strong> and <strong>the only</strong> are different complete indicators. “Only people who get a bicycle win this raffle” and “The only people who win this raffle are people who get a bicycle” both keep the rule: winning proves getting a bicycle.</p></details>
